The company describes itself as building \"the world's first AI-native hospital information system\" — a ground-up rebuild of hospital IT infrastructure rather than an incremental improvement on legacy systems. The founding team includes doctors, engineers, designers, and hospital operations executives. Lunar is backed by Andreessen Horowitz, Better Tomorrow Ventures, Crossover VC, Rogue Capital, and SNR. The company appears to be early-stage with 11–50 employees (per Wellfound) and remains largely in stealth mode, with very limited public information about specific product capabilities, customer deployments, or feature details.\n     9→\n    10→Lunar specifically targets **smaller hospitals**, positioning its cloud platform as an accessible alternative to the large enterprise EHR vendors. The product spans inpatient, emergency department, and ambulatory settings per its ONC certification description. The company's public messaging emphasizes AI, automation, and modern data architecture as differentiators.\n    11→\n    12→## Product: Lunar Cloud Platform\n    13→\n    14→CHPL IDs: 11698\n    15→\n    16→### What It Is\n    17→\n    18→The Lunar Cloud Platform is a cloud-hosted, comprehensive hospital information system (HIS) designed to serve as the core operational backbone for health systems. It is positioned as an all-in-one platform rather than a modular system — covering clinical, financial, and operational functions across the hospital. The certified module appears to be the platform itself, not a subset of a larger product.\n    19→\n    20→The platform is certified for a broad range of ONC criteria (30+ criteria) spanning clinical data management (a)(1)–(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14), transitions of care (b)(1)–(b)(2), patient access (e)(1), (e)(3), clinical quality measures (c)(1)–(c)(3), public health reporting (h)(1), and FHIR-based API access (g)(7), (g)(9), (g)(10). This is a broad certification profile consistent with a full-featured EHR, not a point solution.\n    21→\n    22→The product was certified on September 17, 2025 (version 2.6), making it a very recently certified product.\n    23→\n    24→### Users & Market\n    25→\n    26→**Target users**: Doctors, nurses, pharmacists, lab technicians, coders, billers, supply chain staff, and patients — per Lunar's own marketing language. The SED intended user description explicitly calls out \"US-based hospitals, clinics, and health systems in an inpatient, ED, and ambulatory setting.\"\n    27→\n    28→**Target market**: Smaller hospitals. Lunar positions itself as an alternative to the legacy enterprise EHR vendors (Epic, Oracle Health/Cerner, MEDITECH) that dominate the large health system market.\n    29→\n    30→**Customer information**: No specific customer deployments are publicly disclosed. Job listings reference \"partner health systems\" and implementation work, suggesting the product is in early deployment or pilot stages. The company's stealth posture makes it unclear how many live sites exist.\n    31→\n    32→**Company size**: Small (11–50 employees per Wellfound). This is a startup, not an established vendor.\n    33→\n    34→**No third-party reviews found**: The product does not appear on G2, Capterra, KLAS, or other review platforms. This is consistent with a pre-market or very early-market product.\n    35→\n    36→### Modules & Functionality\n    37→\n    38→Lunar's public information is deliberately vague about specific modules. The company describes its platform as \"powering everything\" across the hospital but does not publish a module list or feature breakdown on its website. What we can piece together from multiple sources:\n    39→\n    40→**Clinical (inferred from certifications and job listings)**:\n    41→- The ONC certification covers CPOE for medications (a)(1), CPOE for lab orders (a)(2), CPOE for diagnostic imaging (a)(3), drug-drug/drug-allergy interaction checks (a)(4), demographics (a)(5), family health history (a)(12), and implantable device list (a)(14). This implies the product supports core clinical workflows: ordering, documentation, medication management, and clinical decision support.\n    42→- Job listings for \"Clinical Workflow Informaticist\" and \"Fractional Clinical Nursing Informaticist\" confirm active work on clinical workflows and nursing informatics.\n    43→\n    44→**Revenue cycle / billing (inferred from job listings)**:\n    45→- A \"Fractional Revenue Cycle Specialist\" role is listed, and the company repeatedly mentions \"coders\" and \"billers\" as platform users. This strongly suggests the platform includes revenue cycle management functionality — coding, billing, and potentially claims processing.\n    46→\n    47→**Pharmacy**:\n    48→- \"Pharmacists\" are explicitly listed as users. Combined with (a)(1) CPOE for medications and (a)(4) drug interaction checks, the platform appears to support pharmacy workflows.\n    49→\n    50→**Lab**:\n    51→- \"Lab technicians\" are listed as users. Combined with (a)(2) CPOE for lab orders, the platform supports laboratory ordering and likely results management.\n    52→\n    53→**Supply chain**:\n    54→- \"Supply chain\" staff are listed as users, suggesting the platform includes supply chain or materials management capabilities — unusual for an EHR and more typical of a full hospital information system.\n    55→\n    56→**Patient access**:\n    57→- Certified for (e)(1) view/download/transmit, meaning there is a patient-facing portal or access mechanism.\n    58→\n    59→**Interoperability**:\n    60→- FHIR R4 API via EMR Direct's HealthToGo/Interoperability Engine (endpoint at `api.healthtogo.me`). The API is described as read-only. This is a third-party interoperability layer, not a proprietary API.\n    61→- Certified for Direct messaging (h)(1), transitions of care (b)(1)–(b)(2), and clinical information reconciliation (b)(11).\n    62→\n    63→**AI / automation**:\n    64→- The company's core differentiator is AI integration. They describe the platform as \"AI-native\" and emphasize \"smarter automation\" and \"sensible machine learning.\" No specific AI features are detailed publicly.\n    65→\n    66→### Data & Content\n    67→\n    68→Based on the certification criteria and public descriptions, the Lunar Cloud Platform stores and manages:\n    69→\n    70→- **Clinical data**: Patient demographics, medication lists, allergy lists, problem lists, vital signs, lab orders and results, diagnostic imaging orders, implantable device records, family health history, clinical notes (implied by CPOE and CDS certifications).\n    71→- **Medication data**: Prescription data, drug interaction records, medication administration records (implied by (a)(1) and (a)(4) certifications).\n    72→- **Order data**: CPOE for medications, labs, and diagnostic imaging.\n    73→- **Transitions of care**: C-CDAs and care summaries for transitions (b)(1)–(b)(2).\n    74→- **Quality measures**: CQM data per (c)(1)–(c)(3) certifications.\n    75→- **Revenue cycle data**: Billing, coding, and likely claims data (based on job listings for Revenue Cycle Specialist and references to \"coders\" and \"billers\" as users).\n    76→- **Supply chain data**: Likely inventory or materials management data (based on \"supply chain\" staff being listed as users).\n    77→- **Patient portal data**: Patient-accessible health records, and likely messaging (per (e)(1) certification).\n    78→- **Audit/security data**: Certified for multiple (d) criteria including audit logging (d)(1)–(d)(2), authentication (d)(3)–(d)(5), encryption (d)(7)–(d)(9), and trusted connection (d)(12)–(d)(13).\n    79→- **Interoperability data**: FHIR resources, Direct messages, care documents.\n    80→\n    81→**Gaps in knowledge**: The vendor's stealth posture means we have very little detail on the actual data model, specific clinical documentation capabilities (e.g., whether it supports specialized templates, voice dictation, or structured vs. free-text notes), the depth of revenue cycle features, or what supply chain data specifically looks like. The website is intentionally sparse, and there are no user reviews, case studies, or detailed product documentation available publicly.\n    82→\n    83→**Notable**: The FHIR API is provided through EMR Direct's HealthToGo platform (`api.healthtogo.me`), not through a proprietary Lunar API. The company describes itself as building \"the world's first AI-native hospital information system\" — a ground-up rebuild of hospital IT infrastructure rather than an incremental improvement on legacy systems. The founding team includes doctors, engineers, designers, and hospital operations executives. Lunar is backed by Andreessen Horowitz, Better Tomorrow Ventures, Crossover VC, Rogue Capital, and SNR. The company appears to be early-stage with 11–50 employees (per Wellfound) and remains largely in stealth mode, with very limited public information about specific product capabilities, customer deployments, or feature details.\n\nLunar specifically targets **smaller hospitals**, positioning its cloud platform as an accessible alternative to the large enterprise EHR vendors. The product spans inpatient, emergency department, and ambulatory settings per its ONC certification description. The company's public messaging emphasizes AI, automation, and modern data architecture as differentiators.\n\n## Product: Lunar Cloud Platform\n\nCHPL IDs: 11698\n\n### What It Is\n\nThe Lunar Cloud Platform is a cloud-hosted, comprehensive hospital information system (HIS) designed to serve as the core operational backbone for health systems. It is positioned as an all-in-one platform rather than a modular system — covering clinical, financial, and operational functions across the hospital. The certified module appears to be the platform itself, not a subset of a larger product.\n\nThe platform is certified for a broad range of ONC criteria (30+ criteria) spanning clinical data management (a)(1)–(a)(5), (a)(12), (a)(14), transitions of care (b)(1)–(b)(2), patient access (e)(1), (e)(3), clinical quality measures (c)(1)–(c)(3), public health reporting (h)(1), and FHIR-based API access (g)(7), (g)(9), (g)(10). This is a broad certification profile consistent with a full-featured EHR, not a point solution.\n\nThe product was certified on September 17, 2025 (version 2.6), making it a very recently certified product.\n\n### Users & Market\n\n**Target users**: Doctors, nurses, pharmacists, lab technicians, coders, billers, supply chain staff, and patients — per Lunar's own marketing language. The SED intended user description explicitly calls out \"US-based hospitals, clinics, and health systems in an inpatient, ED, and ambulatory setting.\"\n\n**Target market**: Smaller hospitals. Lunar positions itself as an alternative to the legacy enterprise EHR vendors (Epic, Oracle Health/Cerner, MEDITECH) that dominate the large health system market.\n\n**Customer information**: No specific customer deployments are publicly disclosed. Job listings reference \"partner health systems\" and implementation work, suggesting the product is in early deployment or pilot stages. The company's stealth posture makes it unclear how many live sites exist.\n\n**Company size**: Small (11–50 employees per Wellfound). This is a startup, not an established vendor.\n\n**No third-party reviews found**: The product does not appear on G2, Capterra, KLAS, or other review platforms. This is consistent with a pre-market or very early-market product.\n\n### Modules & Functionality\n\nLunar's public information is deliberately vague about specific modules. The company describes its platform as \"powering everything\" across the hospital but does not publish a module list or feature breakdown on its website. What we can piece together from multiple sources:\n\n**Clinical (inferred from certifications and job listings)**:\n- The ONC certification covers CPOE for medications (a)(1), CPOE for lab orders (a)(2), CPOE for diagnostic imaging (a)(3), drug-drug/drug-allergy interaction checks (a)(4), demographics (a)(5), family health history (a)(12), and implantable device list (a)(14). This implies the product supports core clinical workflows: ordering, documentation, medication management, and clinical decision support.\n- Job listings for \"Clinical Workflow Informaticist\" and \"Fractional Clinical Nursing Informaticist\" confirm active work on clinical workflows and nursing informatics.\n\n**Revenue cycle / billing (inferred from job listings)**:\n- A \"Fractional Revenue Cycle Specialist\" role is listed, and the company repeatedly mentions \"coders\" and \"billers\" as platform users. This strongly suggests the platform includes revenue cycle management functionality — coding, billing, and potentially claims processing.\n\n**Pharmacy**:\n- \"Pharmacists\" are explicitly listed as users. Combined with (a)(1) CPOE for medications and (a)(4) drug interaction checks, the platform appears to support pharmacy workflows.\n\n**Lab**:\n- \"Lab technicians\" are listed as users. Combined with (a)(2) CPOE for lab orders, the platform supports laboratory ordering and likely results management.\n\n**Supply chain**:\n- \"Supply chain\" staff are listed as users, suggesting the platform includes supply chain or materials management capabilities — unusual for an EHR and more typical of a full hospital information system.\n\n**Patient access**:\n- Certified for (e)(1) view/download/transmit, meaning there is a patient-facing portal or access mechanism.\n\n**Interoperability**:\n- FHIR R4 API via EMR Direct's HealthToGo/Interoperability Engine (endpoint at `api.healthtogo.me`). The API is described as read-only. This is a third-party interoperability layer, not a proprietary API.\n- Certified for Direct messaging (h)(1), transitions of care (b)(1)–(b)(2), and clinical information reconciliation (b)(11).\n\n**AI / automation**:\n- The company's core differentiator is AI integration. They describe the platform as \"AI-native\" and emphasize \"smarter automation\" and \"sensible machine learning.\" No specific AI features are detailed publicly.\n\n### Data & Content\n\nBased on the certification criteria and public descriptions, the Lunar Cloud Platform stores and manages:\n\n- **Clinical data**: Patient demographics, medication lists, allergy lists, problem lists, vital signs, lab orders and results, diagnostic imaging orders, implantable device records, family health history, clinical notes (implied by CPOE and CDS certifications).\n- **Medication data**: Prescription data, drug interaction records, medication administration records (implied by (a)(1) and (a)(4) certifications).\n- **Order data**: CPOE for medications, labs, and diagnostic imaging.\n- **Transitions of care**: C-CDAs and care summaries for transitions (b)(1)–(b)(2).\n- **Quality measures**: CQM data per (c)(1)–(c)(3) certifications.\n- **Revenue cycle data**: Billing, coding, and likely claims data (based on job listings for Revenue Cycle Specialist and references to \"coders\" and \"billers\" as users).\n- **Supply chain data**: Likely inventory or materials management data (based on \"supply chain\" staff being listed as users).\n- **Patient portal data**: Patient-accessible health records, and likely messaging (per (e)(1) certification).\n- **Audit/security data**: Certified for multiple (d) criteria including audit logging (d)(1)–(d)(2), authentication (d)(3)–(d)(5), encryption (d)(7)–(d)(9), and trusted connection (d)(12)–(d)(13).\n- **Interoperability data**: FHIR resources, Direct messages, care documents.\n\n**Gaps in knowledge**: The vendor's stealth posture means we have very little detail on the actual data model, specific clinical documentation capabilities (e.g., whether it supports specialized templates, voice dictation, or structured vs. free-text notes), the depth of revenue cycle features, or what supply chain data specifically looks like. The website is intentionally sparse, and there are no user reviews, case studies, or detailed product documentation available publicly.\n\n**Notable**: The FHIR API is provided through EMR Direct's HealthToGo platform (`api.healthtogo.me`), not through a proprietary Lunar API. Inc.​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n1\n\n\fLunar EHI Export Overview\nLunar’s EHI Export functionality enables healthcare organizations to extract Electronic Health\nInformation (EHI) from the Lunar Cloud Platform in compliance with §170.315(b)(10) of the ONC\n2015 Edition Cures Update. The platform supports single-patient and bulk-patient data extraction\nin the form of an “export zip” file containing machine-readable files, enabling authorized users to\naccess and manage the designated record set as defined under 45 CFR 164.502.\nThe Lunar Cloud Platform leverages the C-CDA (Consolidated Clinical Document Architecture)\nformat for the majority of patient EHI, adhering to widely recognized industry standards for health\ndata interoperability. The C-CDA XML files provide structured and comprehensive representations\nof clinical information, facilitating the smooth transfer and usability of EHI across systems.\nIn addition to the C-CDA format, certain EHI data - such as images, scanned documents, and\nmultimedia files - are included as comma-separated values (csv) files. These supplemental files\nensure a complete and cohesive representation of the patient’s health information.\nThe content and format of the exported EHI may vary based on:\n\n●​ Lunar Cloud Platform Configuration: Organization-specific settings and workflows.\n●​ Implementation Details: Standard implementations include the Lunar Clinical Suite, Lunar\nRevenue Suite, Lunar Lab Suite, Lunar IT Suite, Lunar Operations Suite, Lunar Analytics\nSuite, and Lunar Patient Suite.\n●​ Software Version: The current version of the Lunar Cloud Platform in use.\n●​ Organizational Practices: Data documentation, input workflows, and system usage.\n\n© 2026 Lunar Systems, Inc.​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n2\n\n\fC-CDA Format Guide\nThe Consolidated Clinical Document Architecture (C-CDA) is a standardized format developed by\nHL7 (Health Level Seven International) to enable the exchange of structured clinical documents\nacross different health systems. It serves as the foundation for the majority of patient data\nexported from the Lunar Cloud Platform.\nC-CDA is an XML-based format that organizes and represents patient health information in a\nmachine-readable, interoperable structure. It provides a standardized way to store and share\ncritical health data, ensuring consistency and compatibility between EHR systems.\nThe Lunar Cloud Platform uses CDA Release 4.1. The official HL7 documentation for C-CDA can be\nfound at the following URL:\nhttps://www.hl7.org/implement/standards/product_brief.cfm?product_id=447\n\n© 2026 Lunar Systems, Inc.​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n3\n\n\fC-CDA Document Sections\nThe following sections outline the core components of a C-CDA document from the Lunar Cloud\nPlatform, each representing a specific aspect of a patient’s health record. These sections are\ndesigned to provide a clear and organized summary of clinical information, including encounters,\nmedications, procedures, and more.\nAdditionally, the last section, Supplemental Data, is a proprietary Lunar data format that\nsupplements a patient’s C-CDA with any additional electronic health information in a series of\ncomma-separated values (csv) files that’s not captured by the standard C-CDA format.\n\n© 2026 Lunar Systems, Inc.​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n4\n\n\fPatient Summary\nThis section includes basic demographic information about the patient, such as name, date of\nbirth, gender, contact details, and identifiers (e.g., MRN). It serves as the foundational metadata\nfor identifying the patient within the document.\n\nExample XML\nThe following example sits within a <recordTarget></recordTarget> set of tags.\n<patientRole>\n<id root=\"1.3.6.1.4.1.38630.2.1.1.117\" extension=\"M02389491\"/>\n<id root=\"2.16.840.1.113883.4.1\" extension=\"888-88-8888\"/>\n<addr>\n<streetAddressLine>3 Lunar Dr</streetAddressLine>\n<city>Moon Township</city>\n<state>CA</state>\n<postalCode>94107</postalCode>\n<country>USA</country>\n</addr>\n<telecom value=\"tel:+12062702651\"/>\n<patient>\n<name>\n<given>Walter</given>\n<family>Boron</family>\n</name>\n<administrativeGenderCode code=\"M\" displayName=\"Male\" codeSystem=\"2.16.840.1.113883.5.1\"\ncodeSystemName=\"AdministrativeGender\"/>\n<birthTime value=\"19321014\"/>\n<maritalStatusCode code=\"M\" displayName=\"Married\" codeSystem=\"2.16.840.1.113883.5.2\"/>\n<raceCode code=\"2106-3\" displayName=\"White\" codeSystem=\"2.16.840.1.113883.6.238\"\ncodeSystemName=\"CDC - Race and Ethnicity\"/>\n<ethnicGroupCode displayName=\"Not Hispanic or Latino\" code=\"2186-5\"\ncodeSystem=\"2.16.840.1.113883.6.238\" codeSystemName=\"Race and Ethnicity - CDC\"/>\n<languageCommunication>\n<languageCode code=\"en\"/>\n</languageCommunication>\n</patient>\n<providerOrganization>\n<id root=\"10e46398-fead-45d2-b2bb-53d769cde8c1\"/>\n<name>Lunar Systems</name>\n<telecom value=\"tel:+18007654321\" use=\"WP\"/>\n<addr>\n<streetAddressLine>1 Lunar Dr</streetAddressLine>\n<city>Moon Township</city>\n<state>CA</state>\n\n© 2026 Lunar Systems, Inc.​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n5\n\n\f<postalCode>94107</postalCode>\n<country>USA</country>\n</addr>\n</providerOrganization>\n</patientRole>\n\nKey elements\nElement\n\nDescription\n\n<patientRole>\n\nWraps the information about the patient\n\n<id>\n\nCaptures unique patient identifiers (e.g., MRN or SSN)\n\n<addr>\n\nSpecifies the patient’s address (e.g., street, city, state, postal code, and\ncountry)\n\n<telecom>\n\nContains the patient’s contact number or email\n\n<patient>\n\nWraps patient-specific details such as name, gender, birth date, and\nethnicity\n\n<name>\n\nContains the patient’s name, with separate elements for given and family\nnames\n\n<administrativeGenderCode>\n\nIndicates the patient’s gender\n\n<birthTime>\n\nSpecifies the patient’s date of birth in YYYYMMDD format\n\n<maritalStatusCode>\n\nRepresents the marital status using a coded value (e.g., Married - M)\n\n<raceCode>\n\nDescribes the patient’s race and ethnicity according to CDC standards\n\n<ethnicGroupCode>\n\nDescribes the patient’s race and ethnicity according to CDC standards\n\n<languageCommunication>\n\nCaptures the patient’s preferred language for communication (e.g., en-US\nfor English)\n\n<providerOrganization>\n\nContains the associated healthcare organization details, such as name,\naddress, and contact\n\n<deceasedInd>\n\nIndicates if the patient is deceased.\n\n<deceasedTime>\n\nSpecifies the patient’s date of death in YYYYMMDD format, if deceased\n\n© 2026 Lunar Systems, Inc.​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n6\n\n\fAllergies and Intolerances\nThe Allergies and Intolerances section lists any known allergies or adverse reactions the patient\nhas experienced to medications, substances, or environmental factors. Each entry includes the\nallergen, reaction type, and severity, helping to prevent adverse clinical events.\n\nExample XML\nThe following example sits within an allergies <section></section> set of tags.\n\nMetadata\nThis defines the section metadata such as the template ID and LOINC code.\n<templateId root=\"2.16.840.1.113883.10.20.22.2.6.1\" extension=\"2015-08-01\"/>\n<code code=\"48765-2\" codeSystem=\"2.16.840.1.113883.6.1\" codeSystemName=\"LOINC\"\ndisplayName=\"Allergies and adverse reactions Document\"/>\n<title>ALLERGIES, ADVERSE REACTIONS, ALERTS</title>\n\nHuman-Readable Text\nThis provides the narrative representation of the allergies in a table format.\n<text>\n<table border=\"1\" width=\"100%\">\n<thead>\n<tr>\n<th>Substance</th>\n<th>Reaction</th>\n<th>Severity</th>\n<th>Status</th>\n</tr>\n</thead>\n<tbody>\n<tr>\n<td><content>Latex</content></td>\n<td><content>Rash</content></td>\n<td><content>Moderate</content></td>\n<td><content>Active</content></td>\n</tr>\n</tbody>\n</table>\n</text>\n\n© 2026 Lunar Systems, Inc.​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n7\n\n\fCoded Entries\nThis contains the structured data for each allergy or adverse reaction. <entry> contains details\nabout the allergy, including substance, severity, and the effective time.\n<entry typeCode=\"DRIV\">\n<observation classCode=\"OBS\" moodCode=\"EVN\">\n<templateId root=\"2.16.840.1.113883.10.20.22.4.7\"/>\n<id root=\"12345\" extension=\"Allergy1\"/>\n<code code=\"ASSERTION\" codeSystem=\"2.16.840.1.113883.5.4\"/>\n<statusCode code=\"completed\"/>\n<effectiveTime>\n<low value=\"20241201\"/>\n</effectiveTime>\n<value displayName=\"Propensity to adverse reaction to drug\" code=\"419511003\" xsi:type=\"CD\"\ncodeSystem=\"2.16.840.1.113883.6.96\" codeSystemName=\"SNOMED CT\"/>\n<participant typeCode=\"CSM\">\n<participantRole classCode=\"MANU\">\n<playingEntity classCode=\"MMAT\">\n<code code=\"7980\" codeSystem=\"2.16.840.1.113883.6.88\" codeSystemName=\"RxNorm\"\ndisplayName=\"Penicillin G\"/>\n</playingEntity>\n</participantRole>\n</participant>\n<entryRelationship typeCode=\"MFST\">\n<observation classCode=\"OBS\" moodCode=\"EVN\">\n<code code=\"271807003\" displayName=\"Rash\" codeSystem=\"2.16.840.1.113883.6.96\"\ncodeSystemName=\"SNOMED-CT\"/>\n<value xsi:type=\"CD\" code=\"6736007\" displayName=\"Moderate\" codeSystem=\"2.16.840.1.113883.6.96\"/>\n</observation>\n</entryRelationship>\n</observation>\n</entry>\n\nKey elements in Coded Entries\nElement\n\nDescription\n\n<entry>\n\nRepresents the allergy or adverse reaction entry\n\n<observation>\n\nContains details about the allergy\n\n<statusCode>\n\nIndicates the status (e.g., active, completed)\n\n<effectiveTime>\n\nSpecifies the time of occurrence/documentation\n\n<code>\n\nCoded representation of the allergen or reaction\n\n© 2026 Lunar Systems, Inc.​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n8\n\n\f<value>\n\nCoded representation of the reaction severity\n\n<entryRelationship>\n\nLinks related observations, such as severity\n\n© 2026 Lunar Systems, Inc.​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n9\n\n\fProblem List\nThis section contains the patient’s active and resolved medical problems, diagnoses, and\nconditions. wrapper for the Problem Concern\n\n<code>\n\nDescribes the concern or problem type\n\n<statusCode>\n\nIndicates the status (e.g., active, completed)\n\n<effectiveTime>\n\nSpecifies the timeframe for the problem\n\n<statusCode>\n\nIndicates the status (e.g., active, completed)\n\n<entryRelationship>\n\nLinks the concern act to the problem observation\n\n<observation>\n\nCaptures the detailed clinical problem or diagnosis\n\n<value>\n\nDescribes the problem using standardized coding systems\n\n© 2026 Lunar Systems, Inc.​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n12\n\n\fHistory of Plan\nThe Treatment Plan Section contains information regarding planned care, treatments, or\nprocedures intended to address a patient’s health conditions. Each <entry> represents a structured plan\nof care, including any scheduled treatments or recommendations.\n<entry>\n<procedure moodCode=\"RQO\" classCode=\"PROC\">\n<templateId root=\"2.16.840.1.113883.10.20.22.4.41\" extension=\"2022-06-01\"/>\n<id root=\"12345678-abcd-1234-efgh-9876543210\"/>\n<code code=\"467771000124109\" codeSystem=\"2.16.840.1.113883.6.96 \"codeSystemName=\"SNOMED CT\"\ndisplayName=\"Assistance with application for food pantry program\"/>\n<text>\n<reference value=\"Plan_78c442e0-c37a-4caf-9fa1-62b7e02727e1__0\"/>\n</text>\n<statusCode code=\"active\"/>\n<effectiveTime value=\"20241201\"/>\n</procedure>\n</entry>\n\n© 2026 Lunar Systems, Inc.​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n41\n\n\fKey elements in Coded Entries\nElement\n\nDescription\n\n<entry>\n\nWraps the structured treatment plan data\n\n<procedure>\n\nRepresents the planned clinical action or service\n\n<templateId>\n\nIdentifies the CCDA Plan of Care Activity Procedure template\n\n<statusCode>\n\nIndicates the status of the intervention (e.g., “active” or “planned”)\n\n<effectiveTime>\n\nRepresents the time when the planned treatment is scheduled to begin or\napplies\n\n<entryRelationship>\n\nLinks the treatment act to a specific observation (e.g., planned procedure\ndetails)\n\n<observation>\n\nContains details of the planned care, such as a specific procedure or\ntherapy\n\n<code>\n\nSNOMED CT code describing the planned intervention\n\n<text>\n\nHuman-readable reference linking the structured entry back to the\nnarrative Plan of Care section\n\n© 2026 Lunar Systems, Inc.​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n42\n\n\fClinical Notes\nThe Clinical Notes Section provides detailed narrative summaries or observations documented\nduring a patient’s care. These notes can include various types of clinical documentation such as\nprogress notes, emergency department notes, discharge summaries, and more.\n\nExample XML\nThe following example sits within the clinical notes <section></section> set of tags.\n\nMetadata\nThis defines the section metadata, such as the template ID and LOINC code.\n<templateId root=\"2.16.840.1.113883.10.20.22.2.65\" extension=\"2016-11-01\"/>\n<code code=\"11506-3\" codeSystem=\"2.16.840.1.113883.6.1\" codeSystemName=\"LOINC\" displayName=\"Clinical\nNotes Section\"/>\n<title>Clinical Notes Section</title>\n\nHuman-Readable Text\nThis provides the narrative representation of the clinical notes in a table format.\n<text>\n<table border=\"1\" width=\"100%\">\n<thead>\n<tr>\n<th>Type</th>\n<th>Summary</th>\n<th>Written by:</th>\n<th>Date</th>\n</tr>\n</thead>\n<tbody>\n<tr>\n<td>Progress Note</td>\n<td ID=\"Note_38751ca6-d1dd-4a33-80e8-ec65346b7867\">\nMs Alice Newman seems to be developing high fever and hence we recommend her to get admitted to a\nnearby hospital using the provided referral.\n</td>\n<td>Albert Davis MD</td>\n<td>2015-06-22 10:00 EST</td>\n</tr>\n</tbody>\n</table>\n</text>\n© 2026 Lunar Systems, Inc.​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n43\n\n\fCoded Entries\nThis contains the structured data for the clinical notes. Export Overview\n    57→Lunar’s EHI Export functionality enables healthcare organizations to extract Electronic Health\n    58→Information (EHI) from the Lunar Cloud Platform in compliance with §170.315(b)(10) of the ONC\n    59→2015 Edition Cures Update. The platform supports single-patient and bulk-patient data extraction\n    60→in the form of an “export zip” file containing machine-readable files, enabling authorized users to\n    61→access and manage the designated record set as defined under 45 CFR 164.502.\n    62→The Lunar Cloud Platform leverages the C-CDA (Consolidated Clinical Document Architecture)\n    63→format for the majority of patient EHI, adhering to widely recognized industry standards for health\n    64→data interoperability. The C-CDA XML files provide structured and comprehensive representations\n    65→of clinical information, facilitating the smooth transfer and usability of EHI across systems.\n    66→In addition to the C-CDA format, certain EHI data - such as images, scanned documents, and\n    67→multimedia files - are included as comma-separated values (csv) files. These supplemental files\n    68→ensure a complete and cohesive representation of the patient’s health information.\n    69→The content and format of the exported EHI may vary based on:\n    70→\n    71→●​ Lunar Cloud Platform Configuration: Organization-specific settings and workflows.\n    72→●​ Implementation Details: Standard implementations include the Lunar Clinical Suite, Lunar\n    73→Revenue Suite, Lunar Lab Suite, Lunar IT Suite, Lunar Operations Suite, Lunar Analytics\n    74→Suite, and Lunar Patient Suite.\n    75→●​ Software Version: The current version of the Lunar Cloud Platform in use.\n    76→●​ Organizational Practices: Data documentation, input workflows, and system usage.\n    77→\n    78→© 2026 Lunar Systems, Inc.​\n    79→\n    80→​\n    81→\n    82→​\n    83→\n    84→​\n    85→\n    86→​\n    87→\n    88→​\n    89→\n    90→​\n    91→\n    92→​\n    93→\n    94→​\n    95→\n    96→​\n    97→\n    98→2\n    99→\n   100→\fC-CDA Format Guide\n   101→The Consolidated Clinical Document Architecture (C-CDA) is a standardized format developed by\n   102→HL7 (Health Level Seven International) to enable the exchange of structured clinical documents\n   103→across different health systems. It serves as the foundation for the majority of patient data\n   104→exported from the Lunar Cloud Platform.\n   105→C-CDA is an XML-based format that organizes and represents patient health information in a\n   106→machine-readable, interoperable structure. It provides a standardized way to store and share\n   107→critical health data, ensuring consistency and compatibility between EHR systems.\n   108→The Lunar Cloud Platform uses CDA Release 4.1. The official HL7 documentation for C-CDA can be\n   109→found at the following URL:\n   110→https://www.hl7.org/implement/standards/product_brief.cfm?product_id=447\n   111→\n   112→© 2026 Lunar Systems, Inc.​\n   113→\n   114→​\n   115→\n   116→​\n   117→\n   118→​\n   119→\n   120→​\n   121→\n   122→​\n   123→\n   124→​\n   125→\n   126→​\n   127→\n   128→​\n   129→\n   130→​\n   131→\n   132→3\n   133→\n   134→\fC-CDA Document Sections\n   135→The following sections outline the core components of a C-CDA document from the Lunar Cloud\n   136→Platform, each representing a specific aspect of a patient’s health record. These sections are\n   137→designed to provide a clear and organized summary of clinical information, including encounters,\n   138→medications, procedures, and more.\n   139→Additionally, the last section, Supplemental Data, is a proprietary Lunar data format that\n   140→supplements a patient’s C-CDA with any additional electronic health information in a series of\n   141→comma-separated values (csv) files that’s not captured by the standard C-CDA format.\n   142→\n   143→© 2026 Lunar Systems, Inc.​\n   144→\n   145→​\n   146→\n   147→​\n   148→\n   149→​\n   150→\n   151→​\n   152→\n   153→​\n   154→\n   155→​\n   156→\n   157→​\n   158→\n   159→​\n   160→\n   161→​\n   162→\n   163→4\n   164→\n   165→\fPatient Summary\n   166→This section includes basic demographic information about the patient, such as name, date of\n   167→birth, gender, contact details, and identifiers (e.g., MRN). It serves as the foundational metadata\n   168→for identifying the patient within the document.\n   169→\n   170→Example XML\n   171→The following example sits within a <recordTarget></recordTarget> Dr</streetAddressLine>\n   206→<city>Moon Township</city>\n   207→<state>CA</state>\n   208→\n   209→© 2026 Lunar Systems, Inc.​\n   210→\n   211→​\n   212→\n   213→​\n   214→\n   215→​\n   216→\n 242→<patientRole>\n   243→\n   244→Wraps the information about the patient\n   245→\n   246→<id>\n   247→\n   248→Captures unique patient identifiers (e.g., MRN or SSN)\n   249→\n   250→<addr>\n   251→\n   252→Specifies the patient’s address (e.g., street, city, state, postal code, and\n   253→country)\n   254→\n   255→<telecom>\n   256→\n   257→Contains the patient’s contact number or email\n   258→\n   259→<patient>\n   260→\n   261→Wraps patient-specific details such as name, gender, birth date, and\n   262→ethnicity\n   263→\n   264→<name>\n   265→\n   266→Contains the patient’s name, with separate elements for given and family\n   267→names\n   268→\n   269→<administrativeGenderCode>\n   270→\n   271→Indicates the patient’s gender\n   272→\n   273→<birthTime>\n   274→\n   275→Specifies the patient’s date of birth in YYYYMMDD format\n   276→\n   277→<maritalStatusCode>\n   278→\n   279→Represents the marital status using a coded value (e.g., Married - M)\n   280→\n   281→<raceCode>\n   282→\n   283→Describes the patient’s race and ethnicity according to CDC standards\n   284→\n   285→<ethnicGroupCode>\n   286→\n   287→Describes the patient’s race and ethnicity according to CDC standards\n   288→\n   289→<languageCommunication>\n   290→\n   291→Captures the patient’s preferred language for communication (e.g., en-US\n   292→for English)\n   293→\n   294→<providerOrganization>\n   295→\n   296→Contains the associated healthcare organization details, such as name,\n   297→address, and contact\n   298→\n   299→<deceasedInd>\n   300→\n   301→Indicates if the patient is deceased.\n   302→\n   303→<deceasedTime>\n   304→\n   305→Specifies the patient’s date of death in YYYYMMDD format, if deceased\n   306→\n   307→© 2026 Lunar Systems, Inc.​\n   308→\n   309→​\n   310→\n   311→​\n   312→\n   313→​\n   314→\n   315→​\n   316→\n   317→​\n   318→\n   319→​\n   320→\n   321→​\n   322→\n   323→​\n   324→\n   325→​\n   326→\n   327→6\n   328→\n   329→\fAllergies and Intolerances\n   330→The Allergies and Intolerances section lists any known allergies or adverse reactions the patient\n   331→has experienced to medications, substances, or environmental factors. Each entry includes the\n   332→allergen, reaction type, and severity, helping to prevent adverse clinical events.\n   333→\n   334→Example XML\n   335→The following example sits within an allergies 495→\n   496→9\n   497→\n   498→\fProblem List\n   499→This section contains the patient’s active and resolved medical problems, diagnoses, and\n   500→conditions.   612→Element\n   613→\n   614→Description\n   615→\n   616→<entry>\n   617→\n   618→Represents a problem or concern entry\n   619→\n   620→<act>\n   621→\n   622→Serves as a wrapper for the Problem   648→<value>\n   649→\n   650→Describes the problem using standardized coding systems\n   651→\n   652→© 2026 Lunar Systems, Inc.​\n   653→\n   654→​\n   655→\n   656→​\n   657→\n   658→​\n   659→\n 787→\n   788→Represents an individual medication administration record\n   789→\n   790→<substanceAdministration>\n   791→\n   792→Captures the structured details of the medication use\n   793→\n (EHI) from the Lunar Cloud Platform in compliance with §170.315(b)(10) of the ONC\n2015 Edition Cures Update. The platform supports single-patient and bulk-patient data extraction\nin the form of an “export zip” file containing machine-readable files, enabling authorized users to\naccess and manage the designated record set as defined under 45 CFR 164.502.\nThe Lunar Cloud Platform leverages the C-CDA (Consolidated Clinical Document Architecture)\nformat for the majority of patient EHI, adhering to widely recognized industry standards for health\ndata interoperability. The C-CDA XML files provide structured and comprehensive representations\nof clinical information, facilitating the smooth transfer and usability of EHI across systems.\nIn addition to the C-CDA format, certain EHI data - such as images, scanned documents, and\nmultimedia files - are included as comma-separated values (csv) files. These supplemental files\nensure a complete and cohesive representation of the patient’s health information.\nThe content and format of the exported EHI may vary based on:\n\n●​ Lunar Cloud Platform Configuration: Organization-specific settings and workflows.\n●​ Implementation Details: Standard implementations include the Lunar Clinical Suite, Lunar\nRevenue Suite, Lunar Lab Suite, Lunar IT Suite, Lunar Operations Suite, Lunar Analytics\nSuite, and Lunar Patient Suite.\n●​ Software Version: The current version of the Lunar Cloud Platform in use.\n●​ Organizational Practices: Data documentation, input workflows, and system usage.\n\n© 2026 Lunar Systems, Inc.​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n2\n\n\fC-CDA Format Guide\nThe Consolidated Clinical Document Architecture (C-CDA) is a standardized format developed by\nHL7 (Health Level Seven International) to enable the exchange of structured clinical documents\nacross different health systems. It serves as the foundation for the majority of patient data\nexported from the Lunar Cloud Platform.\nC-CDA is an XML-based format that organizes and represents patient health information in a\nmachine-readable, interoperable structure. It provides a standardized way to store and share\ncritical health data, ensuring consistency and compatibility between EHR systems.\nThe Lunar Cloud Platform uses CDA Release 4.1. The official HL7 documentation for C-CDA can be\nfound at the following URL:\nhttps://www.hl7.org/implement/standards/product_brief.cfm?product_id=447\n\n© 2026 Lunar Systems, Inc.​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n3\n\n\fC-CDA Document Sections\nThe following sections outline the core components of a C-CDA document from the Lunar Cloud\nPlatform, each representing a specific aspect of a patient’s health record. These sections are\ndesigned to provide a clear and organized summary of clinical information, including encounters,\nmedications, procedures, and more.\nAdditionally, the last section, Supplemental Data, is a proprietary Lunar data format that\nsupplements a patient’s C-CDA with any additional electronic health information in a series of\ncomma-separated values (csv) files that’s not captured by the standard C-CDA format.\n\n© 2026 Lunar Systems, Inc.​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n4\n\n\fPatient Summary\nThis section includes basic demographic information about the patient, such as name, date of\nbirth, gender, contact details, and identifiers (e.g., MRN). It serves as the foundational metadata\nfor identifying the patient within the document.\n\nExample XML\nThe following example sits within a <recordTarget></recordTarget> set of tags.\n<patientRole>\n<id root=\"1.3.6.1.4.1.38630.2.1.1.117\" extension=\"M02389491\"/>\n<id root=\"2.16.840.1.113883.4.1\" extension=\"888-88-8888\"/>\n<addr>\n<streetAddressLine>3 Lunar Dr</streetAddressLine>\n<city>Moon Township</city>\n<state>CA</state>\n<postalCode>94107</postalCode>\n<country>USA</country>\n</addr>\n<telecom value=\"tel:+12062702651\"/>\n<patient>\n<name>\n<given>Walter</given>\n<family>Boron</family>\n</name>\n<administrativeGenderCode code=\"M\" displayName=\"Male\" codeSystem=\"2.16.840.1.113883.5.1\"\ncodeSystemName=\"AdministrativeGender\"/>\n<birthTime value=\"19321014\"/>\n<maritalStatusCode code=\"M\" displayName=\"Married\" codeSystem=\"2.16.840.1.113883.5.2\"/>\n<raceCode code=\"2106-3\" displayName=\"White\" codeSystem=\"2.16.840.1.113883.6.238\"\ncodeSystemName=\"CDC - Race and Ethnicity\"/>\n<ethnicGroupCode displayName=\"Not Hispanic or Latino\" code=\"2186-5\"\ncodeSystem=\"2.16.840.1.113883.6.238\" codeSystemName=\"Race and Ethnicity - CDC\"/>\n<languageCommunication>\n<languageCode code=\"en\"/>\n</languageCommunication>\n</patient>\n<providerOrganization>\n<id root=\"10e46398-fead-45d2-b2bb-53d769cde8c1\"/>\n<name>Lunar Systems</name>\n<telecom value=\"tel:+18007654321\" use=\"WP\"/>\n<addr>\n<streetAddressLine>1 Lunar Dr</streetAddressLine>\n<city>Moon Township</city>\n<state>CA</state>\n\n© 2026 Lunar Systems, Inc.​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n5\n\n\f<postalCode>94107</postalCode>\n<country>USA</country>\n</addr>\n</providerOrganization>\n</patientRole>\n\nKey elements\nElement\n\nDescription\n\n<patientRole>\n\nWraps the information about the patient\n\n<id>\n\nCaptures unique patient identifiers (e.g., MRN or SSN)\n\n<addr>\n\nSpecifies the patient’s address (e.g., street, city, state, postal code, and\ncountry)\n\n<telecom>\n\nContains the patient’s contact number or email\n\n<patient>\n\nWraps patient-specific details such as name, gender, birth date, and\nethnicity\n\n<name>\n\nContains the patient’s name, with separate elements for given and family\nnames\n\n<administrativeGenderCode>\n\nIndicates the patient’s gender\n\n<birthTime>\n\nSpecifies the patient’s date of birth in YYYYMMDD format\n\n<maritalStatusCode>\n\nRepresents the marital status using a coded value (e.g., Married - M)\n\n<raceCode>\n\nDescribes the patient’s race and ethnicity according to CDC standards\n\n<ethnicGroupCode>\n\nDescribes the patient’s race and ethnicity according to CDC standards\n\n<languageCommunication>\n\nCaptures the patient’s preferred language for communication (e.g., en-US\nfor English)\n\n<providerOrganization>\n\nContains the associated healthcare organization details, such as name,\naddress, and contact\n\n<deceasedInd>\n\nIndicates if the patient is deceased.\n\n<deceasedTime>\n\nSpecifies the patient’s date of death in YYYYMMDD format, if deceased\n\n© 2026 Lunar Systems, Inc.​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n6\n\n\fAllergies and Intolerances\nThe Allergies and Intolerances section lists any known allergies or adverse reactions the patient\nhas experienced to medications, substances, or environmental factors. Each entry includes the\nallergen, reaction type, and severity, helping to prevent adverse clinical events.\n\nExample XML\nThe following example sits within an allergies <section></section> set of tags.\n\nMetadata\nThis defines the section metadata such as the template ID and LOINC code.\n<templateId root=\"2.16.840.1.113883.10.20.22.2.6.1\" extension=\"2015-08-01\"/>\n<code code=\"48765-2\" codeSystem=\"2.16.840.1.113883.6.1\" codeSystemName=\"LOINC\"\ndisplayName=\"Allergies and adverse reactions Document\"/>\n<title>ALLERGIES, ADVERSE REACTIONS, ALERTS</title>\n\nHuman-Readable Text\nThis provides the narrative representation of the allergies in a table format.\n<text>\n<table border=\"1\" width=\"100%\">\n<thead>\n<tr>\n<th>Substance</th>\n<th>Reaction</th>\n<th>Severity</th>\n<th>Status</th>\n</tr>\n</thead>\n<tbody>\n<tr>\n<td><content>Latex</content></td>\n<td><content>Rash</content></td>\n<td><content>Moderate</content></td>\n<td><content>Active</content></td>\n</tr>\n</tbody>\n</table>\n</text>\n\n© 2026 Lunar Systems, Inc.​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n7\n\n\fCoded Entries\nThis contains the structured data for each allergy or adverse reaction. <entry> contains details\nabout the allergy, including substance, severity, and the effective time.\n<entry typeCode=\"DRIV\">\n<observation classCode=\"OBS\" moodCode=\"EVN\">\n<templateId root=\"2.16.840.1.113883.10.20.22.4.7\"/>\n<id root=\"12345\" extension=\"Allergy1\"/>\n<code code=\"ASSERTION\" codeSystem=\"2.16.840.1.113883.5.4\"/>\n<statusCode clinical problem or diagnosis\n\n<value>\n\nDescribes the problem using standardized coding systems\n\n© 2026 Lunar Systems, Inc.​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n\n​\n12\n\n\fHistory of Plan\nThe Treatment Plan Section contains information regarding planned care, treatments, or\nprocedures intended to address a patient’s health conditions. 10 * 1024 * 1024 }).toString();\n\n// Split into pages (pdftotext uses form feed \\f)\nconst pages = rawText.split(\"\\f\").map((p) => p.trim()).filter(Boolean);\n\n// Remove page footers\nconst cleanPage = (text: string) =>\n  text\n    .replace(/© 2026 Lunar Systems, Inc\\.\\s*​?/g, \"\")\n    .replace(/\\n\\s*\\d+\\s*$/g, \"\")\n    .trim();\n\n// Known C-CDA section names from the table of contents\nconst sectionNames = [\n  \"Patient Summary\",\n  \"Allergies and Intolerances\",\n  \"Problem List\",\n  \"History of Medication Use\",\n  \"Laboratory/Diagnostic Results\",\n \"Assessments\",\n  \"Treatment Plan\",\n  \"Clinical Notes\",\n  \"Payers\",\n  \"Participant\",\n  \"Supplemental Data\",\n];\n\ninterface KeyElement {\n  element: string;\n  description: 0; p < pages.length; p++) {\n      const cleaned = cleanPage(pages[p]);\n      // Look for the section name as a heading (at start of a cleaned page block)\n      if (\n        cleaned.startsWith(name) ||\n        cleaned.includes(`\\n${name}\\n`) ||\n        cleaned.match(new RegExp(`^\\\\s*${name.replace(/[/()]/g, \"\\\\$&\")}\\\\s*$`, \"m\"))\n      ) {\n        // Make sure we're past the table of contents\n        if (p > 1) {\n          startPage = p;\n          break;\n        }\n      }\n    }\n\n    if (startPage !== -1) {\n      // End page is the start of next section - 1, or last page\n      let endPage = pages.length - 1;\n      if (i + 1 < sectionNames.length) {\n        const nextName = sectionNames[i + 1];\n        for (let p = startPage + 1; p < cleaned.match(new RegExp(`^\\\\s*${nextName.replace(/[/()]/g, \"\\\\$&\")}\\\\s*$`, \"m\"))\n          ) {\n            endPage = p - 1;\n            break;\n          }\n        }\n      }\n      results.push({ name, startPage, endPage });\n    }\n  }\n\n  return results;\n}\n\n// Extract key elements table from section text\nfunction extractKeyElements(text: string): KeyElement[] {\n  const elements: KeyElement[] = [];\n  // Look for \"Key elements\" section\n  const keyIdx = text.indexOf(\"Key elements\");\n  if (keyIdx === -1) return elements;\n\n  const afterKey = text.substring(keyIdx);\n  // Match element/description pairs - elements are XML tags in angle brackets\n  const lines = afterKey.split(\"\\n\").map((l) => l.trim()).filter(Boolean);\n\n  let currentElement = \"\";\n  for (const line of lines) {\n    if (line === \"Element\" || line === \"Description\" || line.startsWith(\"Key elements\")) continue;\n    // Check if this looks like an XML element name\n    const elemMatch = line.match(/^<([a-zA-Z]+(?:\\s*\\(.*?\\))?)>$/);\n    if (elemMatch) {\n      currentElement = line;\n    } else if (currentElement && line.length > 5) {\n      elements.push({ element: currentElement, description: line });\n      currentElement = \"\";\n    }\n  }\n\n  return elements;\n}\n\n// Extract templateId from section text\nfunction extractTemplateId(text: string): string | null {\n  const match = text.match(/<templateId root=\"([^\"]+)\"(?:\\s+extension=\"([^\"]+)\")?/);\n  if (match) return match[2] ? `${match[1]} (${match[2]})` : match[1];\n  return null;\n}\n\n// Extract LOINC code from section metadata\nfunction extractLoincCode(text: string): { code: string | null; display: string | null } {\n  const match = text.match(/<code code=\"([^\"]+)\".*?codeSystem=\"2\\.16\\.840\\.1\\.113883\\.6\\.1\".*?displayName=\"([^\"]+)\"/);\n  if (match) return { code: match[1], display: match[2] };\n  return { code: null, display: null };\n}\n\n// Extract supplemental data tables\nfunction extractSupplemental(text: string): SupplementalCategory[] {\n  const categories: SupplementalCategory[] = [];\n  const categoryNames = [\"Documents\", \"Orders\", \"Charges\"];\n\n  for (let i = 0; i < categoryNames.length; i++) {\n    const catName = categoryNames[i];\n    const catIdx = text.indexOf(`\\n${catName}\\n`);\n    if (catIdx === -1) continue;\n\n    const nextCatIdx =\n      i + 1 < categoryNames.length\n        ? text.indexOf(`\\n${categoryNames[i + 1]}\\n`, catIdx + 1)\n        : text.length;\n    const catText = text.substring(catIdx, nextCatIdx);\n\n    const fields: SupplementalField[] = [];\n    const lines = catText.split(\"\\n\").map((l) => l.trim()).filter(Boolean);\n\n    // Parse field/description/datatype triples\n    let idx = 0;\n    // Skip header lines\n    while (idx < lines.length && !lines[idx].match(/^Field$/)) idx++;\n    idx++; // skip \"Field\"\n    while (idx < lines.length && (lines[idx] === \"Description\" || lines[idx] === \"Field Data Type\")) idx++;\n\n    while (idx < lines.length) {\n      const field = lines[idx];\n      if (!field || field === catName || field === \"Field\" || field === \"Description\") break;\n      const desc = lines[idx + 1] || \"\";\n      const dtype = lines[idx + 2] || \"\";\n      // Validate: data type should be a known type\n      if ([\"string\", \"boolean\", \"datetime\", \"date\", \"dict\", \"int\", \"float\", \"uuid\", \"array\"].includes(dtype)) {\n        fields.push({ field, description: desc, dataType: dtype });\n        idx += 3;\n      } else {\n        // Multi-line description - try to find the data type\n        let found = false;\n        for (let j = idx + 2; j < Math.min(idx + 5, lines.length); j++) {\n          if ([\"string\", \"boolean\", \"datetime\", \"date\", \"dict\", \"int\", \"float\", \"uuid\", \"array\"].includes(lines[j])) {\n            fields.push({\n              field,\n              description: lines.slice(idx + 1, j).join(\" \"),\n              dataType: lines[j],\n            });\n            idx = j + 1;\n            found = true;\n            break;\n          }\n        }\n        if (!found) idx++;\n      }\n    }\n\n    categories.push({ category: catName, fields });\n  }\n\n  return categories;\n}\n\n// \" \").trim() : \"\";\n\n  const templateId = extractTemplateId(sectionText);\n  const loinc = extractLoincCode(sectionText);\n  const keyElements = extractKeyElements(sectionText);\n\n  sections.push({\n    name: sp.name,\n    description,\n    templateId,\n    loincCode: loinc.code,\n    loincDisplay: loinc.display,\n    hasExampleXml: sectionText.includes(\"Example XML\"),\n    keyElements,\n    pageRange: `${sp.startPage + 1}-${sp.endPage + 1}`,\n  });\n}\n\n// Extract supplemental data\nconst supplementalStartPage = sectionPages.find((s) => s.name === \"Supplemental These contain the majority of clinical data.\n2. **Supplemental CSV files** — for data that doesn't fit into C-CDA format, organized into three categories: documents, orders, and charges. completeness.\n\n## Export Coverage Assessment\n\n### Data Domain Coverage\n\n**Clearly covered in C-CDA:**\n- Demographics, contacts, identifiers (Patient Summary)\n- Allergies and adverse reactions\n- Problem list / diagnoses\n- Medications (history of use)\n- Lab/diagnostic results\n- Procedures\n- Social history (smoking, tribal affiliation, etc.)\n- Functional status\n- Mental/cognitive status\n- Vital signs\n- Encounters (visit history with diagnoses)\n- Immunizations\n- Care team members\n- Clinical assessments\n- Treatment plans / care plans\n- Clinical notes (progress notes, discharge summaries, etc.)\n- Insurance/payer information\n- Participant relationships (family/legal contacts)\n\n**Covered in supplemental CSV:**\n- **Documents** — appears to capture documents, images, scanned materials, and multimedia that don't map to C-CDA. The `json_data` and `application_data` dict fields suggest this is a catch-all for structured data that doesn't fit the C-CDA model. This is a good sign for (b)(10) completeness.\n- **Orders** — all clinical orders beyond what's in the procedures/medications sections. Includes investigation times, urgency, and linked visit data.\n- **Charges/billing** — revenue codes, procedure codes, modifiers, NDC codes, quantities, rates, totals, diagnosis linkages. This is genuine billing data, not just insurance information. This is a critical (b)(10) domain and Lunar has documented it.\n\n**Potentially missing or unclear:**\n- **Medication administration records (MAR)** — The C-CDA section covers \"History of Medication Use\" but the examples show medication orders rather than detailed administration events (when exactly each dose was given by a nurse). MARs may be captured via the supplemental Orders or Documents CSV, but this isn't explicitly stated.\n- **Implantable device data** — Lunar is certified for (a)(14) implantable device list, but there is no dedicated C-CDA section or supplemental CSV for device records. Devices may be included under the Procedures section or the supplemental Documents CSV, but this is not documented.\n- **Referrals** — Care plans mention referral-like content, but there's no explicit referral section.\n- **Supply chain data** — The product research identifies supply chain staff as users, but no supply chain data appears in the export. This is likely NOT EHI (operational data), so this is not a gap.\n- **Images and attachments as binary files** — The overview mentions \"images, scanned documents, and multimedia files\" are included as supplemental CSV data. However, the CSV schema only has text-based fields (text_data, json_data, application_data). It's unclear whether actual image binaries (DICOM, JPEG, etc.) are exported or only metadata about them.\n- **Family health history** — Certified for (a)(12), but no dedicated section in the export. May be under Social History or Participant sections but not clearly documented.\n\n### Export Format & Standards\n\nThe choice of **C-CDA XML** as the primary format is well-established and interoperable. C-CDA is a recognized industry standard backed by HL7, and the document references CDA Release 4.1. The template IDs and LOINC codes for each section are standard C-CDA identifiers — this is not a proprietary format masquerading as C-CDA.\n\nThe **supplemental CSV** approach for data beyond C-CDA is pragmatic. CSV with JSON columns is not a formal standard, but it's computable and accessible. The three categories (documents, orders, charges) represent a genuine attempt to capture data that C-CDA was never designed to hold — particularly billing charges with revenue codes, modifiers, and NDC codes.\n\n**This is NOT a (g)(10) FHIR API repackaged as (b)(10).** The export uses C-CDA (not FHIR), includes billing/charges data far beyond USCDI, and has a separate supplemental CSV mechanism for non-clinical data. This is clearly a dedicated (b)(10) implementation, not a cosmetic relabeling of a FHIR Bulk Data endpoint.\n\nThe format is appropriate for the data. A third party could reconstruct a meaningful patient record from the C-CDA plus supplemental CSVs, though the JSON-in-CSV pattern for the supplemental data would require documentation of the JSON schemas (which are not provided — the `json_data`, `application_data`, and `data` fields are described only as \"dict\" type without schema details).\n\n### Documentation Quality\n\n**Strengths:**\n- Well-organized 54-page PDF with clear table of contents\n- Every C-CDA section has: description, metadata, complete XML example, human-readable text example, coded entry example, and key elements table\n- Sample data is realistic (patient names, addresses, clinical scenarios)\n- Template IDs, LOINC codes, and code systems are correctly specified\n- The document explicitly addresses the (b)(10) requirement and references the designated record set (45 CFR 164.502)\n\n**Weaknesses:**\n- **No sample export files** — there are XML examples in the PDF, but no downloadable sample C-CDA file or sample CSV files that a developer could test against\n- **Supplemental CSV schemas are sparse** — the three CSV tables have field names and data types, but fields like `json_data`, `application_data`, and `data` are typed as \"dict\" with no documentation of their internal structure. What keys does `json_data` contain for a document? What structure does `data` have for an order? This is a significant gap for implementability.\n- **No complete C-CDA example** — while each section has an XML snippet, there is no full sample C-CDA document showing how all sections compose together\n- **No export instructions** — the document describes the format but not how to trigger the export. There's no user guide for performing the export (which button to click, which API endpoint to call, what permissions are needed)\n- **No schema files** — no XSD, no formal schema definition for the supplemental CSVs\n- **Single document** — all documentation is in one PDF with no machine-readable artifacts (no schema files, no sample data files, no OpenAPI specs)\n\n### Structure & Completeness\n\n**Field-level documentation granularity:**\n- C-CDA sections: element names + descriptions (moderate granularity). No data types or cardinality specified beyond what's implicit in C-CDA.\n- Supplemental CSV: field names + descriptions + data types (good granularity for flat fields, but \"dict\" and \"array\" fields lack internal structure documentation).\n\n**Coded fields:** The C-CDA sections reference standard code systems (SNOMED-CT, LOINC, RxNorm, CVX, NCI Thesaurus, NUCC Taxonomy) with example codes. Value sets are not explicitly enumerated but can be inferred from C-CDA standard constraints.\n\n**Relationships between entities:** The C-CDA structure inherently defines relationships through nesting (entries within sections, observations within organizers). The supplemental CSVs link to visits via UUID fields, but there's no documentation of how the CSV `visit` UUIDs correspond to encounters in the C-CDA.\n\n**Versioning:** The document is dated \"Jan 2026\" and tagged to platform version 2.6.
